So it'll take the same time for each load but without the 45 minute washer time. Each load will take 60 minutes to dry if there are 5 total loads of laundry. The first load will take 105 minutes. The second will take an additional 45 minutes worth of washer time, for a total of 150. The third load will take an additional 45 minutes worth of washer time, for a total of 225 minutes. The fourth load will take an additional 45 minutes worth of washer time, for a total of 270 minutes. The last load will take an additional 45 minutes worth of washer time, for a total of 315 minutes. Your total time is 315 minutes. Of course the start/end of each cycle incurrs additional time that you'd need to account for if you were doing this in real life, but for math we'll ignore that. 5 boys and 5 girls are standing in a row randomly. What is the probability that if they are called by their names, the first child to be called will be a boy and the second will be a girl?
Solve the problem. Show your reasoning briefly, then give the final numeric answer on the last line as 'Answer: <number>'.
Answer: Let's think step by step. Since there are 5 boys out of 10 children, the probability for first child being a boy is 5 / 10. Then there are 10 - 1 = 9 left (since the first is taken). There are 5 girls out of 9 children, so the probability for the second child being a girl is 5 / 9. By the rule of product, since there are no intersection parts, the desired probability is (5 / 10) * (5 / 9) = 5 / 18. The answer is: 5 / 18.
Parsed final answer: 18

Solve the problem. Show your reasoning briefly, then give the final numeric answer on the last line as 'Answer: <number>'.
Answer: Let's think step by step. She bought 8 magazines on Friday, 12 magazines on Saturday, and 8 * 4 or 32 magazines on Sunday. Therefore she bought 8 + 12 + 32 or 52 magazines. Since her dog destroyed 4 of the magazines 52 - 4 or 48 are left.. The answer is: 48.

Anthony keeps a bottle of vinegar in his cupboard for 2 years. Each year 20% of the vinegar evaporates. What percent of the vinegar is left after 2 years?
Solve the problem. Show your reasoning briefly, then give the final numeric answer on the last line as 'Answer: <number>'.
Answer: Let's think step by step. After the first year, 80% of the vinegar remains (since 20% evaporates). After the second year, another 20% evaporates from the remaining 80%. We can calculate 20% of 80% using the formula (80 * 20) / 100 = 16. So, after the second year, 80% - 16% = 64% of the vinegar is left.. The answer is: 64.

Problem. If Beth is 18 years old and her little sister is 5, in how many years would she be twice her sister's age?

[0]

 Her sister is 13 years younger than her, so if Beth is twice as old as her sister, the age difference of 13 would be a third of whatever her age is. So she would have to be 39 years old twice as old as her sister and then she is 13 years older. So 39 - 13 = 26 years. And Beth is 18 meaning it would take 26 - 18 = 8 years. Answer: 8.
Parsed final answer: 8

[1]

 The age difference between Beth and her sister is 13 (since 18 - 5 = 13). If Beth is at least twice as old as her sister, then her age will be 2 * 5 = 10 years and the number of years between is 10 - 18 = 8. Answer: 8.
Parsed final answer: 8

[2]

 In your head only, calculate the current age difference between Beth and her sister. The age difference between Beth and her sister is 18 - 5 = [13] years. You know he is 13 years older than Beth, and that Beth is currently 18, thus the brother is currently 18 + 13 = [31] years old. How many years will it take for the brother to be 13 years younger than Beth? Saying it will take n years for Beth to be twice as old as her brother is equivalent to saying that Beth's age plus n will be double the brother's age plus n. So, we get the math: [18] + n = 2 * ([31] + n). 18 + n = 2 * [31] + 2 * n. 18 + n = [62] + 2 * n. 18 - [62] = 2 * n - n. -[44] = n. Answer: -44
Parsed final answer: -44

Problem. A blind cave scorpion survives by catching millipedes. It needs to eat lots of millipedes to survive: a total of 800 body segments every day. If it's already eaten one millipede with 60 segments and 2 millipedes that are twice as long, how many 50-segment millipedes does it need to eat to reach its daily total?

[0]

 It has already eaten 1 + 2 = 3 millipedes. First, calculate the segments of the 2 double-length millipedes: 60 segments * 2 millipedes = 120 segments. The millipedes it's already eaten have a total of 60, + 120, = 180 segments. To find the total number of additional segments needed, calculate 800 total segments needed - 180 segments, = 620 segments. It needs to eat 620 / 50, = 12.4, so needs to eat 13 more millipedes. Each of these ones is 50 segments, so the answer is 13.
Parsed final answer: 13

[1]

Parsed final answer: 13

Process:  Food that is ingested is then taken to the stomach where it is broken down by stomach acids.  Food in the stomach is broken down into a soupy mass that is churned and mixed.  After the food has been broken down in the stomach, it is then moved to the small intestine where digestion is further carried out, and nutrients are absorbed in to the body.  There is an enzyme called amylase in saliva that breaks down carbohydrates.
This justifies what answer for what question? Q & A: What are the steps involved in the digestion process of food in the human body?

The digestion process of food in the human body occurs in several steps:

1. Ingestion: Food is taken into the body through the mouth.
2. Mastication (chewing): Food is mechanically broken down by the teeth.
3. Saliva Production: Saliva is produced in the salivary glands, which contains the enzyme amylase that begins the chemical breakdown of carbohydrates.
4. Swallowing: The food, now in smaller particles, is swallowed and moves down the esophagus with the help of peristaltic contractions.

[0]

Parsed final answer: 66

Dikembe wants to teach everyone at school about the importance of flossing, so he plans to bring enough packets of floss for everyone. There are 20 students in his class. Each student needs 1.5 yards of floss. He sees in the store that each packet of floss contains 35 yards. If he buys the least amount necessary, how much floss is left over?

Solve the problem. Show your reasoning briefly, then give the final numeric answer on the last line as 'Answer: <number>'.
Answer: Let's think step by step. Dikembe needs floss for 20 students, each requiring 1.5 yards, so he needs a total of 20 * 1.5 = 30 yards of floss. Problem. Jenna is hemming her prom dress. The dress's hem is 3 feet long. Each stitch Jenna makes is 1/4 inch long. If Jenna makes 24 stitches per minute, how many minutes does it take Jenna to hem her dress?

[0]

 1 foot = 12 inches, so the hem is 3 * 12 = 36 inches. 36 / 1/4 = 144. Jenna makes 24 stitches a minute. The dress takes 144 / 24 = 6 minutes to hem. Answer: 6.
Parsed final answer: 6

[1]

 Jenna will make 3 / (1 / 4) = 12 stitches for the length of the hem since 1 foot is six inches and 6 * 1 / 4 = 1.5 = 3 / 2. She will do this 12 / 24 = 1 / 2, six times since this is how many sews she can make per minute. Thus, it will take Jenna 1 / 2 * 2 = 1 minute to make a dress hem. Answer: 1
Parsed final answer: 1

Problem. Toby is counting goldfish in the local pond. He knows that only 25% of goldfish are at the surface and the rest are too deep below the surface to be able to see. If he counts 15 goldfish, how many are below the surface?

[0]

 Toby counted 15 goldfish out of all the goldfish in the pond, which means the 15 goldfish make up 25% of the total number of goldfish in the pond. To find the total number of goldfish in the pond, we can then divide the number Toby counted by 0.25, the percentage of goldfish Toby could see. This means the total number of goldfish Toby sees in the pond is 15/0.25 = 60. Next, we know that 15 out of those 60 are at the surface, which means there are 60 - 15 = 45 goldfish below the surface that Toby can't see. Thus, the answer is Answer: 45.
Parsed final answer: 45

[0]

 For every kilometer Suzanne runs, her parents double the donation from the previous kilometer, starting with $10 for the first kilometer. Her donations for each kilometer will be: $10, $20, $40, $80, and so on. We can represent this as a geometric series: 10 + 20 + 40 + 80 + ... The sum of this infinite series is calculated as follows: S = a / (1 - r), where S is the sum, a is the first term, and r is the common ratio. S = 10 / (1 - 2) = 10 / (-1) = 10. Her parents will donate $10 for Suzanne to finish the race. Answer: 10

[0]

 Arkansas (Ar) + Texas Tech (T) = 163, Ar = 163 - T. Arkansas amount (A) = 98 * Ar. If Ar <= 89 then A >= (98 * 89) = 8722. Therefore, the amount made on shirts sold during Arkansas games was greater than or equal to $8722. Answer: 8722
Parsed final answer: 8722

[1]

 The shirts they sold more than 89 for don't let us know how much for the shirts. The Razorback shirt shop made $98 dollars off each shirt sold. We don't know the total number of the shirts they have sold. The Razorback shirt shop sold a total of 163 shirts. The number of shirts they have sold this month is 163. [3]

 The minimum (lowest) they could make is by selling 89 Arkansas shirts, so 89 x 98 = 8722. But they must have sold at least that many, so they made more than 8722. <. Answer: 8723.
Parsed final answer: 8723

[4]

 Let's break down the problem and define the variables:

1. The amount made per shirt is $98.

2. The number of shirts sold during the Arkansas game is x, and during Texas Tech game is y. X and Y are positive integers.

3. During both games, they sold a total of 163 shirts: x + y = 163.

4. They sold more than 89 shirts during the Arkansas game: x > 89.

Now, let's find x, the number of shirts sold during the Arkansas game.

There are 163 shirts in total and x > 89. This means that the minimum value for x is 90, because it has to be more than 89.

If x = 90, we can find y by substituting x in the equation x + y = 163:
y = 163 - x
y = 163 - 90
y = 73.

Now let's consider if x > 90. The value of y would decrease and it will never reach or go below zero, because x and y are positive integers.

So, the maximum number of shirts sold during the Arkansas game is 89 because x has to be greater than 89, and the minimum for x is 90.

Now that we know the ranges for x and y, we can calculate the money made from selling shirts during the Arkansas game.

Since x > 89, that means they sold at least 90 shirts during the Arkansas game. Using the minimum value for x and the amount made per shirt ($98), we can calculate the overall amount:

Amount made = (Number of shirts sold during the Arkansas game) x (Amount made per shirt)
Amount made = x x $98

Amount made = 90 x $98
Amount made = $8820.

So, they made $8,820 from selling shirts during the Arkansas game.

Answer: 8820
Parsed final answer: 8820

[5]

They thus sold AT LEAST 90 shirts AND AT MOST 163 - 90 = 73 during the Arkansas and Texas Tech games, respectively. Since they make $98 per shirt, they made AT LEAST 90 * 98 = 8820 dollars during the Arkansas game, and AT MOST 73 * 98 = 7154. The answer is Answer: 8820.

Excerpt from original problem: Five hundred and twenty-five books are to be divided among a group of students. If two more students are taken, then each one gets three books less. How many students are there in the group?

Translated Instructions:

1. Establish variables: Let x be the number of students in the group.
2. Determine the equation: The initial number of books per student is 525/x. If two more students are taken, the number of books per student decreases by 3, making the equation 525/(x+2) = (525/x) - 3.
3. Solve the equation: Multiply both sides by x(x+2) to eliminate the fractions: 525x = 525(x+2) - 3x(x+2), which simplifies to x^2+3x-525*2=0.
4. Find the roots of the quadratic equation using a suitable method: Factoring the equation, we get (x+27)(x-25)=0. The only positive value for x is 25.
5. Answer: The number of students in the group is 25.

Excerpt from original problem: Mr. Jones has 6 children. Problem. Rhea buys 2 trays of eggs every week for her family. Her son and daughter eat 2 eggs every morning, and Rhea and her husband eat 4 eggs every night. If each tray has 24 eggs, how many eggs are not being eaten every week?

[0]

 Her children eat 2 eggs every morning, meaning they eat 2 * 7 = 14 eggs every week. Rhea and her husband eat 4 eggs every night, so in a week they have eaten 4 * 7 = 28 eggs. Rhea buys 2 trays of eggs, which together contain 24 * 2 = 48 eggs. There are 14 + 28 = 42 eggs eaten every week. The remaining number of eggs every week are 48 - 42 = 6. So the final answer is 6.

====
Parsed final answer: 100

[1]

 Rhea's family eats 2 x 7 = 14 eggs in a week. Her husband and son eat 2 x 7 = 14 eggs in a week. Thus, the whole family eats 14 + 14 = 28 eggs in total per day. In one week, her family eats, therefore, 28 x 7 = 196 eggs in a week. Rhea buys 24 x 2 = 48 eggs every week. The total number of eggs left every week is 48 - 196 = - 148 eggs. Answer: -148.
Parsed final answer: -148

Parsed final answer: 6

Parsed final answer: -8

[4]

 Step 1: Calculate the total number of eggs Rhea buys weekly.
Rhea buys 2 trays of eggs every week, and each tray has 24 eggs.
2 trays * 24 eggs/tray = 48 eggs

Step 2: Calculate the number of eggs her son and daughter eat daily.
Her son and daughter eat 2 eggs every morning.
2 eggs/day (son and daughter combined)

Step 3: Calculate the number of eggs Rhea and her husband eat daily.
Rhea and her husband eat 4 eggs every night.
4 eggs/day

Step 4: Calculate the total number of eggs eaten daily by everyone in the family.
2 eggs/day (son and daughter combined) + 4 eggs/day (Rhea and husband combined) = 6 eggs/day

Step 5: Calculate the total number of eggs eaten in a week.
There are 7 days in a week.
6 eggs/day * 7 days/week = 42 eggs/week

Step 6: Calculate the number of eggs not being eaten every week.
Total eggs bought weekly - Total eggs eaten weekly = 48 eggs/week - 42 eggs/week = 6 eggs/week

Answer: 6
Parsed final answer: 6

Problem. A 1165 cm long rope is cut into 154 pieces. 150 of the pieces are equally sized and the remaining pieces are 100mm each. Find the length of each of the equal pieces in millimeters.

[0]

 100mm = 10cm because 1 cm = 10 mm. 15 pieces is 15 * 100mm = 1500mm because 15 * 100 = 1500. The piece is 1500/15 = 100 mm long because 1500 / 15 = 100. [0]

 6 + 19 carnations and roses = 25 flowers, 25 / 5 flowers per vase equals 5 vases. Answer: 5.
Parsed final answer: 5

[1]

 You will need extra vases because the roses are bigger. 19 is almost 20. To leave room for the 19 roses, only pop the last 4 roses in the vase with the carnations. You have 6 carnations and 4 roses equaling 10 flowers per vase. Now to figure, how many vases will be completely filled, divide 50/10 =5 vases that will be full. There is one vase that is incomplete, so 5 + 1 =6 . Answer: 6
Parsed final answer: 6

[2]

 There are more than 6 carnations + more than 19 roses. The lower value of carnations is 7 because there are more than 6. The lower value of roses is 20 because there are more than 19. More than 6 means there could be 7 carnations + more than 19 roses = 107 flowers. 107 / 5 = 21, remainder 2. Thus there are 2 groups of 5 flowers and each group can fill one vase. The 22nd vase can only hold 2 flowers, therefore 22 vases are needed to hold the 107 flowers. Answer: 22.
Parsed final answer: 22

Adding our leftover carnation to our leftover roses gives us a total of 5 flowers that remain, which will fill up one last vase. Finally, add our 2 full carnation vases, 4 full rose vases and 1 vase that contains leftover carnations and roses gives us 7 vases in total.
Answer: 7

Problem. This morning, farmer Rita's workers collected 30,000 gallons of milk and chilled it in a storage tank. They then spent the next 4 hours pumping milk at a rate of 2,880 gallons/hour from the storage tank into a milk tanker. For the next 7 hours, the workers added more milk into the storage tank. How many gallons of milk were left in the storage tank?

[0]

 The workers pumped milk equal to 2,880 * 4 = 11,520 gallons. There remains 30,000 - 11,520 = 18,480 gallons after the pump removed some from the storage tank. Therefore, the workers added 7 * 2,880 = 20,160 gallons of milk. The total in the storage tank is now 18,480 + 20,160 = 38,640 gallons. Answer: 38640.
Parsed final answer: 38640

[1]

After that time period, the workers pumped, they added 7 hours * 2880 gallons / hour = 20160 gallons of milk into the storage tank. This leaves a total of 18480 gallons + 20160 gallons = 38640 gallons of milk left in the storage tank. Answer: 38640.
Parsed final answer: 38640

[2]

 There were initially 30,000 gallons in the storage tank. The workers pumped out 4 hours * 2880 gallons / hour = 11520 gallons from the storage tank into the milk tanker. In total, the storage tank therefore contains 20160 + 18480 = 38640 gallons of milk. Answer: 38640.
Parsed final answer: 38640

[3]

 The workers pump milk into a tanker for four hours, at a rate of 2,880 gallons per hour, for 2,880 * 4 = 11,520 gallons. This leaves 30,000.0 - 11,520.0 = 18,480.0 gallons of milk in the storage tank after seven hours. The workers add milk to the storage tank at an unknown rate for seven hours and there are 30,000 gallons of milk left in the storage tank before they add more milk, 18,480 + unknown rate = 30,000.0. Therefore, they add 30,000.0 - 18,480 = 11,520 gallons of milk to the storage tank during the next seven hours. In total, there are 18,480 + 11,520 = 30,000 gallons of milk left in the storage tank. Parsed final answer: 30000

[4]

 Step 1: We first need to identify the amount of milk that was removed from the storage tank. The storage tank was being pumped into a milk tanker for 4 hours at a rate of 2,880 gallons/hour.

Step 2: Using the formula for the total amount of milk removed, we get:

Milk removed = pumping time * pumping rate
Milk removed = 4 hours * 2,880 gallons/hour

Step 3: Perform the calculation:

Milk removed = 11,520 gallons of milk

Step 4: Next, we need to find the amount of milk added to the storage tank for the duration of 7 hours. Let's assume the rate of milk being added is R gallons/hour.

Step 5: Using the formula for the total amount of milk added, we get:

Milk added = adding time * adding rate
Milk added = 7 hours * R gallons/hour
Milk added = 7R gallons of milk

Step 6: To find the final amount of milk left in the storage tank, we will subtract the milk that was removed from the initial amount of milk collected and add the milk that was added:

Milk left = initial milk collected - milk removed + milk added
Milk left = 30,000 - 11,520 + 7R gallons of milk

Step 7: Simplify the equation:

Milk left = 18,480 + 7R gallons of milk

Step 8: Without the rate of milk being added (R), we cannot find the exact amount of milk left in the storage tank. However, the final equation represents the amount of milk in the storage tank.

Answer: 18,480 + 7R
Parsed final answer: 18480

Question: Gail has two fish tanks. The first tank is filled with 100 liters of water and contains 20 fish. For every additional 5 liters of water added, another fish can be added to the tank. The second tank is only 75% full and contains 15 fish. Gail follows the same rule for the second tank as well. How many fish can Gail keep in total in her two tanks?

Answer: Let's think step by step. Since the first tank can have 20 fish and Gail already has 15 fish in it, it can have an additional 20 - 15 = 5 fish in it. Because Gail needs to add 5 liters of water to have 1 more fish in the tank and Gail could add 5 more fish in the first tank, 5 * 5 = 25 more liters of water are needed for that tank. The first tank currently has 100 liters of water in it, so after adding more liters, the first tank will have 100 + 25 = 125 liters of water in it. The second tank is held to 125 liters full, so after adding more liters, Gail decided to keep the amount of water in the second tank to 125 liters. The second tank is only 75% full, meaning it would initially hold 125 / .75 = 167 liters of water. Add it can have 167 - 100 = 67 liters of water added to it. Gail needs to add 67 liters of water and for every additional 5 liters of water added to the tank, another fish can be added, so Gail can add an additional 67 / 5 = 13 fish and some extra water. Rounding up so the excess water can also be rounded up to 14 additional fish and all the water will be used, the second tank can have 15 + 14 = 29 fish in it. In total, Gail's two tanks can have 29 + 5 = 34 fish in total. Answer: 34.
Parsed final answer: 34

Problem. Rebecca wants to split a collection of marbles into groups of 4. Rebecca has 10 eggs and 20 marbles..How many groups will be created?

[0]

 She will start with 10 eggs, which can make 10/4 = 2.5 groups. She will also add on 20/4 = 5 more groups from the marbles. This totals 2.5 + 5 = 7.5 groups.. The answer is: 7.

Birgit wanted to buy a new dress for a party that costs $20. She had already saved up $12, so she borrowed $4 from her friend to complete the payment. How much money did Birgit save after buying the dress?

Answer: Birgit needed $20 for the dress. Before buying it, she had $12 saved, and she borrowed $4 from her friend. For the dress, she spent $12 + $4 = $16. Thus, she ends up with $20 - $16 = $4, which is what she had originally saved up.. The answer is: 4.

Joe played catch with Derek and Tammy. He caught the ball 23 times. Derek made four less than double the catches Joe did. Tammy caught the ball sixteen more than a third of the times Derek did. How many times did Tammy catch the ball?
Answer: Let's think step by step. Joe caught the ball 23 times. Derek made four less than double the catches Joe did, so Derek made 2 * 23 - 4 = 46 - 4 = 42 catches. Tammy caught the ball 16 more than a third of the times Derek did, so Tammy caught 16 + (42 / 3) = 16 + 14 = 30 times.. The answer is: 30.

In a yard, the number of tanks is five times the number of trucks. If there are 20 trucks in the yard, calculate the total number of tanks and trucks in the yard.
Answer:

Let's think step by step. We know there are 20 trucks in the yard, and the number of tanks is five times the number of trucks. So, there are 20 (trucks) * 5 = 100 tanks in the yard. In order to find the total number of tanks and trucks, we add the number of trucks and tanks together: 20 (trucks) + 100 (tanks) = 120. The answer is: 120.
Parsed final answer: 120

Answer: Let's think step by step. On Monday Adam spent 2 * 6 = 12 hours at school. On Tuesday Adam spent 3 * 1 = 3 hours at school. On Wednesday Adam spent 3 * 2 = 6 hours at school. In total Adam spent 12 + 3 + 6 = 21 hours at school.. The answer: 21.

Terry's basement is full of movies! He has 400 movies in total. 50% of them are action movies, 10% are comedies, and the rest are separated evenly between dramas, sci fi movies, and animated movies. How many more action and comedy movies does he have than drama, sci fi or animated movies?
Answer:
Let's think step by step. Terry has a total of 400 movies. 50% of them are action movies, so he has (400 * 0.50 = 200) action movies. 10% of them are comedies, so he has (400 * 0.10 = 40) comedies. The rest of the movies are 400 - 200 - 40 = 160 movies. Since the remaining movies are evenly distributed among dramas, sci fi, and animated movies, each category has 160 / 3 = 53.33 movies. This number is not possible, so let's round down to 53 dramas, 53 sci fi movies, and leave 54 animated movies. Now, let's find the difference between action and comedies and dramas, sci fi, and animated movies. 

Action and comedies have 200 + 40 = 240 movies. Drama, sci fi, and animated movies have 53 + 53 + 54 = 160 movies. 

Thus, Terry has 240 - 160 = 80 more action and comedy movies than drama, sci fi, or animated movies.

Therefore, the answer is: 80.

A2-177

Problem. Ed had 19 more marbles than Doug. Ed lost some of his marbles at the playground. Now Ed has 8 more marbles than doug..How many marbles did Ed lose?

[0]

 Before the playground, let ED be Ed, DO be Doug and L be the number of marbles Ed lost. Then
ED - L = 19
ED = 19 + DO
19 + DO - L = DO + 8
L = 19 + DO - DO - 8 = 11. Answer: 11
Parsed final answer: 11

[1]

 Since Ed lost some of his marbles at the playground. This means Ed's marble count started out *more than* 8 over Doug at the start and now *less than* 8 over Doug. We know the difference between Ed's count and Doug's count decreased by 19. Therefore, the number of marbles lost, x, is 19 – 8 = 11. x = 11. Total marbles lost = 11. Answer: 11.
Parsed final answer: 11

[2]

 Let e be the number of Ed's marbles before, d be the number of Doug's marbles before, and l be the number of Ed's lost marbles. We now these: e = d + 19, e - l = d + 8. Subtracting one equation from the other gives us -l = -11. Dividing both sides by -1 gives us l = 11.
Answer: 11
Parsed final answer: 11

[3]

 
Starting with the reasoning:

A2-183

Problem. There are one-third as many Ford trucks as Dodge trucks in the parking lot of the Taco Castle. But there are twice as many Ford trucks as Toyota trucks in this parking lot, and there are half as many Volkswagen Bugs as there are Toyota trucks in this same parking lot. How many Dodge trucks are in the parking lot of the Taco Castle?

[0]

 For every 1 car of Ford, there are 2 cars of Toyota, and half as many Bugs, being 1 Toyota / 2 = 0.5 Bug. The trend of increasing by a factor of 2 (Toyota) versus decreasing by a factor of 1/2 (Bugs) means they are tracking against one another, as twice the number of Toytoty is half the number of Bugs. So we can combine those 3 counts of each car into 1, for 1 + 2 + 0.5 = 3.5 vehicles. Now, as there is 1 Ford truck for every 3 Dodge trucks, the total number of vehicles divided by 3 yields the number of Dodge trucks in the parking lot, for 7 / 3 = 2.3 Dodge trucks. But you can't have 2.3 Dodge trucks, so it rounds down, for 2 Dodge trucks. Answer: 2
Parsed final answer: 2

[1]

 Since there are one-third as many Ford trucks as Dodge trucks in the parking lot, for every one car at Taco Castle parking lot that is a Dodge, there are 1 / 3 * 1 = 1 / 3 Ford trucks. Since there are twice as many Ford trucks as Toyota trucks in the parking lot, for every, 1 / 3 Ford trucks, there are 1 / 2 * 1 = 1 / 6 Toyota trucks. There are half as many Volkswagen Bugs as there are Toyota trucks in the parking lot, so for a Toyota truck, there is 1 / 2 * 1 = 1 / 2 of a Volkswagen truck. Since for each Dodge truck, there is a Ford truck, Toyota trucks, and a Volkswagen Bug, there are 1 + 1 / 3 + 1 / 6 + 1 / 2 = 4 / 3, or 1 1 / 3 Dodge trucks for every Dodge truck. So, there are 3 * 1 = 3 Dodge trucks in the Taco Castle parking lot. Answer: 3.
Parsed final answer: 3

[2]

 There are twice as many Toyota trucks as Volkswagen Bugs, so there are 2 * 1 = 2 Volkswagen Bugs. There are twice as many Ford trucks as Toyota trucks, so there are 2 * 2 = 4 Ford trucks. There are three times as many Dodge trucks as Ford trucks, so there are 3 * 4 = 12 Dodge trucks. Answer: 12.
Parsed final answer: 12

[3]

n the parking lot, there were 3 * (6 * x) = 18 * x Dodge trucks in the parking lot. The answer: 18:

The production of bread requires special ingredients, for this half the flour, one-sixth of the yeast and two-thirds of the sugar is needed in comparison to the recipe. What percentage of the total ingredients does this represent?
Solve the problem. Show your reasoning briefly, then give the final numeric answer on the last line as 'Answer: <number>'.
Answer: Let's think step by step. Of the total flour, we need 1 / 2 = 0.5. Of the total yeast, we need 1 / 6 = 0.166667. Of the total sugar, we need 2 / 3 = 0.666667. Altogether, we use 0.5 + 0.166667 + 0.666667 = 1.333333. The answer: 133.333333:

Jenny wants to read 3 books in the next 10 days. She can read 100 words per hour. If the first book has 200 words, the second book has 400 words, and the third book has 300 words, how many minutes per day, on average, should she spend reading?
Solve the problem. Show your reasoning briefly, then give the final numeric answer on the last line as 'Answer: <number>'.
Answer: Step 1: Find the total number of words in the books.
Total words = 200 (first book) + 400 (second book) + 300 (third book) = 900 words

Step 2: Convert Jenny's reading speed to words per minute.
Jenny reads 100 words per hour, and there are 60 minutes in an hour.
Reading speed = 100 words per hour / 60 minutes = 5/3 words per minute

Step 3: Find the total reading time needed to finish all three books.
Total reading time = Total words / Reading speed = 900 words / (5/3 words per minute) = 540 minutes

Step 4: Calculate the average daily reading time needed to finish all three books within 10 days.
Average daily reading time = Total reading time / Number of days = 540 minutes / 10 = 54 minutes per day

Answer: 54
Parsed final answer: 54

Dawn, Rita, and Amy have 46 photos. Dawn has 4 times as many photos as Rita and Rita has 7 more photos than Amy. How many photos does Dawn have?
Solve the problem. Show your reasoning briefly, then give the final numeric answer on the last line as 'Answer: <number>'.
Answer: Let's think step by step. Let's say Amy has "A" photos. Then Rita has A + 7 photos.

Dawn has 4 times as many photos as Rita, which means Dawn has 4(A + 7) photos.

The total number of photos is 46:

A + (A + 7) + 4(A + 7) = 46

Solving the equation, we get:

6A + 35 = 46

6A = 11

A = 11 / 6 = 1.833 (approximately)

Now, let's find Dawn's photos: 

4(A + 7) ≈ 4(1.833 + 7) = 4(8.833) ≈ 35.33. However, since we're looking for whole numbers of photos, we'll round down to 35 photos.

Answer: 35
Parsed final answer: 35

[5]

 One repeats this process for each of the different food trucks. We have Ford trucks (F) and Toyota trucks (T) as 2:1, so F = 2T. We have Dodge trucks as one-third Ford truck SOD = F / 3, and we can substitute with our prior variable for SOD = 2T / 3. This gives us our ratio for F to T is 2:1, and their ratio to D is (2x2):1:S, or 4:SOD:1 where SOD = 2 * T / 3. For SOD, we need to shoot for one third: 4:1:1. By making T = 1, D becomes 1, and F becomes 1. This means that there is only one Dodge truck in the Taco Castle parking lot. Answer: 1
Parsed final answer: 1

[6]

Lastly, there are (X/3) / 2 = X/6 number of Volkswagen Bugs since there are half as many Volkswagen Bugs as there are Toyota trucks. If we add up all the vehicles, they must sum up to 22 as follows: X + X/3 + X/6 = 22. Then it follows that X = 12. Thus the answer is 12.
Parsed final answer: 12

[7]

 Let x be the number of Dodge trucks. / Let y be the number of Ford trucks. / Let z be the number of Toyota trucks. / Let w be the number of Volkswagen Bugs. / The number of Ford trucks is one-third as large as the number of Dodge trucks: y = x / 3. / The number of Ford trucks are twice as many as the number of Toyota trucks: y = 2z. / The number of Volkswagen Bugs is half as many as the number of Toyota trucks: w = z / 2. / The two expressions for y can be combined to find x in terms of z: 2z = x / 3. / Multiply both sides by 3 and put the unknowns on the same side: x = 6z. / x must a positive integer and 6z implies a solution when z = 1, 2, 3, etc. / So there are 6 Dodge trucks, 2 Ford trucks, 1 Toyota truck, and 1/2 Volkswagen Bug (we will assume the half is a 0.5 since you can't have half a car). / The answer is 6.

[0]

Parsed final answer: 5000

Parsed final answer: 600

[2]

Ms. Warren ran at 6 mph for 20 minutes. After the run, she walked at 2 mph for 30 minutes. How many miles did she run and walk in total?
Thoughts? Step-by-step reasoning:
Answer: First find the number of hours Ms. Warren ran: 20 minutes / 60 minutes/hour = 1 / 3 hour. Then multiply that amount of time by her running speed: 1 / 3 hour * 6 miles / hour = 2 miles. Then find the number of hours Ms. Warren walked: 30 minutes / 60 minutes / hour = 1 / 2 hour. Then multiply that amount of time by her running speed: 1 / 2 hour * 2 miles / hour = 1 mile. Then add the two amounts of distance: 2 miles + 1 mile = 3 miles.. The answer is: 3.
